# Generation Compiler V2 — Goal 2 Progress

> Goal 2 implements the Goal 1 plan on `main`. This ledger is updated immediately after each completed implementation or verification step.

## Baseline

- Repository: `[repository-root]`
- Branch: `main`
- Goal 2 baseline SHA: `5b873c5384dad4ac512788ed8223ee5e6ac2885e`
- `origin/main` at start: `fd65a8023849e0079569b5f8320d8748b55fff6e`
- Worktree/index/untracked state at start: clean (`git status --porcelain=v1 -uall` count = 0)
- Started: 2026-07-13 (Asia/Shanghai)
- Sole implementation baseline: this directory's final plan, TP1–TP8, traceability matrix, and AC-01…AC-42.
- Production constraints: no compatibility layer, dual read/write, fallback, unknown wire patch, silent parameter drop/downgrade, or temporary provider bridge. V2 may refactor IPC/UI wiring and internal component structure, but must preserve existing user-visible features, information density, control placement and interaction hierarchy by default. No feature removal, UI simplification or material rearrangement is accepted without a documented technical/product reason, an explicit replacement and focused parity evidence. Destructive epoch-2 activation remains blocked until every currently visible provider/action surface has a complete V2 path.

## Current implementation note

- 2026-07-18: Owner confirmed `link_only` remains a first-class V2 feature. The in-progress V2 composer now distinguishes immutable `managed_file` revisions from immutable `url_reference` records. A link-only registration stores only the user-supplied URL, its SHA-256 digest, reference/revision, media declaration, capture time and provenance; it performs no GET/HEAD, redirect resolution, body injection or file fallback. `link_and_file` has a distinct epoch-2 managed-blob import command and immutable origin provenance, but its actual request input is the captured managed revision. URL values are retained only in draft/snapshot/provenance data, not diagnostic projections. No provider URL codec is claimed yet: current V2 compilers reject a URL reference explicitly until each operation/media encoding has official-contract evidence and an exact-body contract test. This entry is implementation-in-progress and unvalidated.
- 2026-07-18: Activation blocker retained: the current renderer branch projection still contains semantic attachments because retry/edit command plumbing has not yet moved all target-snapshot lookup into the main-process command boundary. Before URL-reference activation, that projection must expose an opaque reference/redacted view only and retry/regenerate must load the exact snapshot server-side. This avoids placing a full user URL in renderer-visible historical projections while preserving exact branch replay.
- 2026-07-18: The first half of that boundary is now wired: `generation-v2:composer:replace-from-answer-snapshot` validates answer/conversation/question ownership in main, decodes the persisted snapshot and populates an edit draft without renderer-supplied historical attachments. The branch projection omits URL and declared media type for `url_reference`; retry remains target-answer-ID based, while regenerate uses the current V2 composer draft as required by current-configuration semantics. This is not yet validated and provider URL codecs remain unavailable.
- 2026-07-18: Official OpenRouter Images documentation rechecked: `/api/v1/images` accepts image edit references only as `input_references[] = { type: "image_url", image_url: { url: "https://..." } }`; it explicitly allows HTTP(S) and data URLs. This is evidence for OpenRouter Images/image only, not a general URL transport rule and not evidence for Chat, document, audio, video, OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, DeepSeek or local contracts. The next implementation unit must bind this exact field only after the V2 Images snapshot/compiler authority can represent a URL reference without routing through managed-byte authority.
- 2026-07-18: In-progress authority split: complete attachment intent resolution now separates managed-byte attachments from URL-reference intents. URL references never receive a byte lease or provider-file descriptor. OpenRouter Images accepts only an included `url_reference` whose media kind is `image`, and maps its persisted snapshot string to its existing dedicated `input_references` request codec. All managed attachments and all other URL media remain compiler-rejected for Images. Exact-body tests are still required before activation.
- 2026-07-18: Corrected an existing V2 Images wire-shape defect before enabling URL references: `input_references` had been encoded as a string array. The codec now emits the documented native object `{ type: "image_url", image_url: { url } }`, accepts only credential-free HTTP(S) URLs and rejects data URLs. The focused request test expectation was updated; it has not run yet.
- 2026-07-18: Images snapshot commits now distinguish resolved managed-file authorities from URL-reference intents. An included managed attachment remains rejected; an included URL reference is allowed only for `mediaKind=image`. Existing non-Images provider checks retain their strict rejection because their operation contracts have no URL-reference codec.
- 2026-07-18: Focused exact-body verification passed: `npx vitest --run src/next/generation-v2/providers/openrouter-images/imageRequestV1.test.ts` — 1 file / 4 tests. It proves the corrected OpenRouter Images `input_references` object wire shape and invalid-reference rejection. No database ABI rebuild was needed and no broad test suite was run.

An intent with no attachments is an affirmative empty-set proof for that resolved intent. The authority does not yet prove persisted snapshot equality, contains no bytes, paths, descriptors or transport encoding, and remains outside production consumers.

It does not issue provider, model, capability, tool-registry, provider-file descriptor, persisted snapshot or transport authority.

## Smoke log

- OpenRouter Images Gate 0 smoke used the existing `electron_safe_storage` credential without persisting it. Dynamic discovery selected `google/gemini-3.1-flash-image`; exact requests tested baseline, `google-ai-studio`, and `google-vertex/global` top-level tags with `n=1`, `resolution=512`, and `aspect_ratio=1:1`.
- All three requests succeeded, cost USD 0.0448255 each (USD 0.1344765 total), and returned generation metadata with the same `provider_name=Google` and endpoint ID `275d7d39-ae50-4df3-8140-5dd69c3ab883`. The descriptor API exposes no endpoint ID, so neither tag produced independently attributable pin evidence; no repeat was needed because all cases were indistinguishable.
- Redacted descriptors, exact bodies/hashes, responses, metadata, cost, conclusion, and OpenAPI SHA-256 are in [`evidence/openrouter-images-provider-tag-smoke-20260714.json`](evidence/openrouter-images-provider-tag-smoke-20260714.json).
- Corrected smoke sent only two documented shapes. AI Studio returned `provider_name=Google AI Studio`, endpoint ID `a5c8267a-c7ec-42d3-9a53-08f34bce6af9`, cost USD 0.045996. Vertex Global returned `provider_name=Google`, endpoint ID `275d7d39-ae50-4df3-8140-5dd69c3ab883`, cost USD 0.0448255. Both were HTTP 200 with one provider response and distinct endpoint IDs; total was USD 0.0908215.
- Corrected exact bodies/hashes, redacted responses/metadata, current descriptors, OpenAPI SHA-256 `abaf90acc89dc3a2b4cd8824afcbf87734c8d0a5f4429ea85dca0d9eb02e353b`, and final routing verdict are in [`evidence/openrouter-images-provider-only-smoke-20260714.json`](evidence/openrouter-images-provider-only-smoke-20260714.json).
- LM Studio qualification used local `0.4.19+2`, CLI commit `9902c3a`, runtime `llama.cpp-win-x86_64-nvidia-cuda12-avx2@2.24.0`, and two local Qwen models. All eleven `/v1/responses` requests were HTTP 200, set `store:false`, omitted `previous_response_id`, and generated no external request or fee.
- Full item replay passed for text multi-turn, two branches, reasoning items and `function_call`/`function_call_output`. The restart audit recorded parent/child PIDs and a persisted-prefix hash before the fresh process replay. The provider stream emitted deltas and one final `response.completed`; Starverse terminal coordination remains an implementation acceptance test. Exact bodies/hashes and complete local response/SSE fields are in [`evidence/lmstudio-openresponses-compliance-20260714.json`](evidence/lmstudio-openresponses-compliance-20260714.json).
- Packaged identity smoke rebuilt Electron/Vite output and both Windows unpacked/NSIS targets. Builder explicitly logged `file=package.json ("build" field)`; the new executable and installer names and VersionInfo use `Starverse`. The current unpacked binary was launched only with a newly created temporary `--user-data-dir`; it reported packaged mode and `Starverse`, honored the override, then closed and deleted the temporary profile without touching shared production data.

## Risks and blockers

- Packaged identity reconciliation, OpenRouter Images routing/request-pin plus strict `n:1` response decoding, LM Studio Responses-first binding/request/continuation, DeepSeek stable endpoint/thinking-history, and Anthropic display semantics are closed for bounded implementation. LM Studio native terminal SSE decoding remains blocked on formal terminal-schema freeze and coordinator acceptance. The Owner has resolved the OpenRouter profile/ID, OpenAI continuation, generic/local fixed-protocol, OpenRouter Beta web, one-attempt transport, and image-continuation policies; their executable routes and acceptance evidence remain pending. Multi-image and `image_generation.text_chunk` are still request-time blocked because their lossless output association is not frozen. No implementation may infer beyond these explicit policies.
- Resolved identity decision: production owns exactly `appId=io.github.guxinghai.starverse`, `productName=Starverse`, package name `starverse-client`. The prior builder placeholders and `com.starverse.desktop` test fixture are not identities to migrate or recognize. `.dev`/`.e2e` alter only Electron/OS application identity. Ordinary launches share `%APPDATA%\Starverse` through an explicit pre-`getPath(userData)` directory creation and `setPath`, not unreliable name-derived inheritance; an explicit non-empty `--user-data-dir` is preserved for bounded smoke/diagnostic isolation. Epoch manifest, ownership and reset checks always use the canonical production appId.
- The packaged Windows native layer now proves the cross-process lease, bounded transition I/O, deterministic crash-temp cleanup, fixed-target handle-relative deletion, strict reset inventory, config replacement, exact epoch root/marker identity and coordinator execution through `epoch_root_created`; the exact Electron default-session adapter is staged but not wired. The private real-`safeStorage` initializer now proves atomic fresh schema/manifest/app identity/scope-envelope creation and strict complete-state reopening without production activation. Destructive cutover remains blocked on restricted runtime credential slot/revision authority, remaining coordinator phases/integration, startup recovery and first/second-start acceptance; ordinary Node filesystem helpers remain forbidden as a fallback.
- Anthropic's exact current model × thinking × effort × sampling × web-tool matrix and Gemini Interactions continuation fields require fresh official-evidence fixtures before those contracts can be enabled; this is evidence work, not a reason to widen or fall back.
- Current official evidence closes most of the Anthropic matrix and proves `thinking.display` is a formal capability-gated field. Older basic web-search combinations without exact evidence remain unavailable.
- Resolved LM Studio decision: this tested endpoint binds `lmstudio-openresponses`. V2 must send `store:false`, never send `previous_response_id`, persist/replay the complete ordered native item union, and use a native Responses SSE decoder. `/api/v1/chat` is forbidden for ordinary multi-turn; `lmstudio-openai-chat-completions` is only the qualification-failure alternative for another endpoint, never a runtime fallback.
- LM Studio qualification failures are classified: auth/connect/timeout/5xx/model/runtime/cancel/inconclusive failures block and leave the endpoint unbound. Only a repeatable native-item contract failure on a healthy runtime permits a separately and explicitly initiated Chat Completions qualification; never in the same transaction and never automatically.
- Current production code does not yet satisfy that binding: it omits forced `store:false`, reduces Responses input to role/text, and sends Responses SSE through the Chat Completions mapper. The successful provider smoke authorizes the V2 contract, not the current implementation.
- LM Studio's 2026-07-15 local SSE supplement closes reasoning and function-call success-event evidence, but not provider `response.failed`/`response.incomplete`. The max-token probe still emitted `response.completed`, and invalid model failed as HTTP 400 before SSE. A complete closed decoder and runtime registration remain blocked until the formal OpenAI-compatible terminal schema and Starverse exactly-once terminal coordinator fixtures are frozen; no observed-text-only compatibility decoder will be introduced.
- OpenRouter Images binding command activation is additionally gated on a real V2 first-party profile authority. Current legacy endpoint metadata, official-URL validator, catalog descriptor/scope, credential service and public identity brand do not bind an epoch-2 credential scope to a revisioned Images profile. Existing binding rows are also `repository_decoded_unverified` and cannot bootstrap that authority. No initial bind, rebind or same-tag revalidation command may persist until a private repository/registry-issued profile fact exists.
- The profile-authority prerequisite cannot be satisfied by adding a static ID alone. Owner must freeze whether one versioned OpenRouter first-party endpoint profile covers Chat and Images or Images owns a separate profile, plus its canonical immutable profile ID. Runtime issuance additionally requires an epoch scope-key authority, a strict epoch-2 credential vault with no legacy migration/plaintext fallback, and a credential-mutation lease/revalidation boundary. The current contract registry remains `definition_only` and its Images OpenAPI evidence has drifted, so it cannot be promoted to execution authority. No caller-issuable profile brand or guessed legacy ID will be added.
- Resolved OpenRouter Images contract: selection belongs to the user and lookup key is `(credentialScopeId, modelId, image_generate)`, using the canonical V2 domain operation without an alias. A valid persisted binding remains; a sole eligible unbound descriptor auto-binds; multiple eligible return `OPENROUTER_IMAGE_PROVIDER_SELECTION_REQUIRED`. Candidate sorting is display-only. Missing/incomplete/hard-expired binding stale-rejects; changed intent unsupported by the bound descriptor returns `BOUND_ENDPOINT_CAPABILITY_MISMATCH`. User rebind plus a new command is required. Exact `provider.only`/`allow_fallbacks:false` and providerSlug/allowlisted options are mandatory, with atomic invalid-option cleanup on binding change.
- Current facts support but do not replace Owner choices: provider generation POSTs perform no automatic retry; OpenAI Responses has no native continuation artifact and replays only generic role/text history; Gemini Interactions image requests explicitly reject history; image regenerate is only question-level sibling generation.
- Current startup constructs and migrates `Store(config.json)` before `app.whenReady`, then opens `<userData>/chat.db`, runs legacy schema/ensure helpers, opens `<userData>/debug/generation-raw.sqlite`, registers IPC/window/jobs, and never owns an epoch marker/lock/journal. Epoch V2 must move config/service construction after a successful coordinator and must never call legacy credential read/migration APIs while filtering.
- The exact preservable records are `providerCredentials.v1.{openrouter,openai_responses,google_ai_studio,anthropic,deepseek}` with valid provider key, version 1, `electron_safe_storage`, non-empty ciphertext, valid timestamp, and successful decrypt. Plaintext fallback, legacy plaintext keys, catalog HMAC, compatible credentials, custom endpoints/secrets, and backups are deletion-only.
- DeepSeek's endpoint and thinking/tool-choice conflicts are closed by Owner decision. Stable V2 owns exactly `https://api.deepseek.com` + `/chat/completions` and `/models`, never appends/probes/falls back to `/v1`, and treats `/beta` as an independently selected contract. Thinking may send function tools, but an omitted `tool_choice` must remain absent and every explicit value must reject before compilation with `DEEPSEEK_THINKING_EXPLICIT_TOOL_CHOICE_UNVERIFIED`; disabled thinking uses the formal Chat schema.
- Any official-contract conflict, reset path outside the managed epoch root, credential outside the closed preservation whitelist, or explicit semantic intent that a provider codec cannot express is a stop condition.
- OpenRouter Images native response/SSE activation is stopped on verified OpenAPI drift: current SHA-256 is `9a36929ad445b27a7010e11b474e8577c0c9b6f6570d67638db285b09adecdd8`, while the reviewed registry definition remains pinned to `abaf90acc89dc3a2b4cd8824afcbf87734c8d0a5f4429ea85dca0d9eb02e353b`. Re-freeze must decide `text_chunk` phases, multi-image streaming identity, completed-without-DONE, known-event extra fields, empty buffered output and omitted `media_type`; no decoder or registry activation may guess them.
- `better-sqlite3` ABI must be switched deliberately: Node before DB/Vitest acceptance; Electron only for the final Electron smoke.
- Fresh epoch database crash acceptance is logical, not a file-size promise. Current hard-kill evidence happened to leave a zero-byte main file and a 512-byte rollback journal at all three injected stages; recovery correctness is the absence of an accepted partial schema/identity/envelope state after SQLite reopens, regardless of future main/journal/WAL sizes.

## Remaining work

- The prior zero-activation frontier was reopened by the Owner identity decision and the bounded packaged-identity reconciliation is complete. Further epoch activation still requires the following real authorities; unrelated registry/schema work must not persist caller self-report or choose an unresolved execution contract.

1. Complete the remaining prepared-request/ledger and provider-native artifact codecs/repositories; operation/snapshot and attempt CAS persistence are complete but still dormant. Add only provider/continuation schema surfaces proven necessary by closed typed codecs. The private fresh-file initializer already atomically creates and verifies the exact schema/app/root identity and real-`safeStorage` scope-key envelope with zero production activation. Next implement monotonic runtime credential slot/revision authority and the atomic answer + snapshot + operation + chosen/head/hide command transaction. Only after those authorities pass may the outer coordinator invoke the initializer, open the runtime credential store, advance remaining phases, integrate startup recovery/default-session reset and prove packaged first/second-start behavior. Exact prepared request bytes remain an immutable compiler/transport/debug-database fact; the main chat database stores only their digest and byte length. Non-Windows remains fail-closed until its own backend exists.
2. After that coordinator/vault fact chain exists, Owner must freeze the OpenRouter first-party profile scope and canonical ID so a revisioned profile fact can bind the epoch-2 credential scope and unlock binding/capability authority.
3. Re-freeze the current OpenRouter Images response/SSE contract and update its reviewed definition/OpenAPI evidence before implementing or activating the native decoder.
4. Obtain and record the remaining package-specific Owner decisions before implementing unrelated provider/continuation/transport slices.
5. Complete epoch-2 coordinator/path cutover before registering any staged repo, settings, IPC or transport; then integrate commands/UI so compiled bytes are exactly the bytes captured and sent.
6. Close every AC with direct code/test/smoke evidence and run the complete gate sequence.

## Historical implementation batch — epoch-2 safety foundation (superseded by the 2026-07-17 native authority batch)

- Batch baseline: `main@07831073023bc8d52bf13b101eead6730de30f46` (recorded 2026-07-14 before edits).
- Scope in progress: one packaged identity authority, builder/runtime/manifest parity, managed-root manifest validation, canonical containment/reparse defense, reset-journal state machine, strict config/credential projection, and crash/idempotency tests.
- Explicit boundary: the Owner-fixed identity authorizes identity reconciliation only. No startup hook, destructive deletion, config replacement, legacy DB path change, or Electron session clearing is authorized until the native coordinator lease/no-follow deletion mechanism and full dry-plan test pass.
- Evidence collection: the project `code_mapper` completed the startup/config/credential/storage trace before implementation; its module-evaluation P0 and exact activation seam are recorded below.
- Read-only startup result: `electron/main.ts` constructs `electron-store`, initializes i18n, migrates/cleans config, and can write corruption backups during module evaluation; `whenReady()` is therefore too late for the complete epoch boundary. They will return only as outputs of one provider-owned compile invocation over a privately branded immutable snapshot and resolver-issued capability/binding; no public assembly API or compatibility patch is retained.

## Current implementation batch — branded snapshot inputs

- Batch baseline: `main@e2958a51` (recorded 2026-07-14 before edits).
- Scope in progress: runtime-verifiable identity/revision/digest primitives, followed by closed sparse/resolved semantic intent, provider binding, capability evidence and immutable snapshot codecs. No ledger or prepared request returns until these branded inputs can feed one provider-owned compile invocation.
- First retained seam: `src/next/generation-v2/domain/identityV2.ts` owns private runtime brands for provider/model/profile/protocol/contract/credential-scope/config/capability/descriptor/asset/tool/operation identities and purpose-bound SHA-256 digests. It rejects empty, padded, controlled, oversized, uppercase or malformed values and does not expose a public construction token.
- Brand boundary: the runtime brand proves that kind/value validation occurred in the current JavaScript realm. It does not prove authorization, database provenance, or that a digest matches separately supplied content; those checks belong to the snapshot codec/repository and same-invocation compiler boundary. JSON projections are deliberately unbranded and must be revalidated rather than cast on load.
- Explicit boundary: no V1 identity, `RuntimeProviderKey`, `ProviderStreamConfig`, generic `Record<string, unknown>`, credential reference, request bytes, wire patch or legacy production import is permitted.
- Provider-contract registry step completed: a reviewed-definition registry is separated from future resolver-issued authority. Its first entry is limited to the frozen OpenRouter Images contract and remains explicitly `definition_only` with `executionAuthority:"none"`; recursive boundary tests prevent every other V2 production module from consuming it. The contract revision is content-addressed by the complete closed definition digest, the registry revision is content-derived, the OpenAPI hash is pinned, and the local provider-only smoke artifact SHA-256 is independently recomputed by tests. Blocked or not-yet-implemented provider contracts are not registered as executable by inference. Five focused files / 23 tests and TypeScript pass; three review findings covering manual revision drift, unbound smoke evidence and ineffective non-execution guards were corrected, and final review reports P0=0/P1=0.
- Binding-record contract-identity step completed: the decoded unverified record now carries both the claimed content-addressed contract definition digest and the claimed registry revision. The strict codec checks lowercase digest shape, `contractRevision = protocolContractId + definitionDigest`, and V1 registry-revision shape, but deliberately proves only structural self-consistency: arbitrary self-consistent values still decode as `decoded_unverified`. A future resolver must look up the real reviewed definition, compare its contract ID/revision/digest, apply the frozen registry-revision policy, validate dynamic endpoint/model evidence, and only then issue a separate private authority brand. This record remains persistence input only and has no resolver/snapshot/compiler authority. Binding/boundary tests pass 2 files / 11 tests and TypeScript passes; the initial overclaim of freshness was corrected, and final review reports P0=0/P1=0.
- Descriptor repository-fact step completed: the inactive epoch-2 schema/repository was replaced rather than compatibility-wrapped. It stores a canonical capability-bearing projection of the last complete successful descriptor set; non-capability `pricing` and raw diagnostic response data are explicitly excluded and must not be reconstructed from this cache. Model and content-derived endpoint-set revision are revalidated on every read. Repository time and a monotonic row generation are separate from content revision; initial insert and conditional update use SQL generation CAS, an immediate transaction and typed lock-conflict normalization, so same-content or cross-connection refreshes cannot overwrite by reusing a digest. Hard expiry is no longer persisted because it must be derived from current V2 settings. Endpoint/rule/value budgets plus a one-MiB UTF-8 repository/read/SQL limit bound cache and history amplification. The decoded result remains `repository_decoded_unverified` and cannot authorize resolver/snapshot/compiler execution. - Freshness-settings persistence step completed: the unused coercive normalizer was deleted. The inactive epoch-2 repository persists the complete pair in one singleton row and uses a separate nondeleted revision clock to prevent CAS ABA when that settings row is missing or repaired. Fresh creation and bypass-corrupt settings collections restore the complete 6h/24h default pair in an immediate transaction; revision exhaustion and invalid clock state fail closed, while whole-pair writes use clock-backed SQL CAS. Trigger-abort tests prove clock/settings rollback for both update and restore-insert failures. Direct external deletion of both repository-owned rows followed by schema reseeding is database-integrity destruction, not a supported recovery path. No legacy `settings_kv`, worker method, IPC or `electron-store` path is imported. - Descriptor-freshness decision and invalidation step completed without transport activation: a strict pure decision owns the exact `< refresh`, `refresh <= age < hard`, `>= hard`, missing-cache and clock-regression boundaries. The endpoint repository now keeps a scope/model/operation generation clock independent of current/history, so a 404 generation-CAS invalidation preserves both diagnostic history and monotonic authority; the next valid refresh advances rather than reusing a generation. Current/clock mismatch, missing authority with retained or future history, exhaustion, stale 404 and lock contention fail closed; history is a one-way corruption witness but never generation authority, and all clock/current/history mutations remain in immediate transactions. It rejects unknown/undefined/null/accessor/sparse/extended/symbol-bearing input, preserves only closed sampling/reasoning/web/image/tool/attachment leaves, canonicalizes set semantics, and brands stable asset/tool references.
- Provider-extension boundary: the common codec currently accepts only `{kind:"none"}`. The first draft's arbitrary OpenRouter `providerSlug/key/scalar` options were removed after P1 review because allowlist validation after persistence is too late. OpenRouter Images options will enter only through its contract package with a fresh selected descriptor, matching providerSlug/revision/digest and typed `allowed_passthrough_parameters`; this is pending and the common intent is not yet a complete provider schema.
- Image semantic boundary: common fields use exact values (`resolution`, exact pixel `size`, `quality`, `background`, `outputCompression`, `format`, `stream`) and a privately branded syntactically normalized positive `W:H`/`auto` aspect ratio, not lossy aliases or an incomplete global ratio enum. The bound endpoint capability decides whether the exact ratio/value is supported. The future persistence codec must explicitly project/read the branded ratio string; it may not serialize the class shape or cast parsed JSON. Provider-specific conflicts remain compiler rejections; no silent conversion is allowed.
- Provider-binding record step in progress: `providerBindingV2.ts` strictly decodes the persisted structural record for non-secret credential scope, provider/model/profile/protocol/contract revision, operation and endpoint evidence. Its exported result is deliberately named `DecodedProviderBindingRecordV2` and carries `trust:"decoded_unverified"`; compiler and snapshot creation must reject it until the capability resolver revalidates every reference/provenance value against registry, descriptor and binding repositories and issues a separate private runtime brand. OpenRouter Images is a bidirectionally closed exact pinned tag/slug/descriptor branch; provider-managed endpoint sets are non-empty, duplicate-free and code-point canonicalized without choosing an endpoint.
- Provider-binding verification: final review reports P0=0/P1=0. Focused V2 identity/intent/binding/boundary tests pass 4 files / 20 tests; the architecture guard forbids any other V2 production module from treating `DecodedProviderBindingRecordV2` as compiler or snapshot authority.
- Resolver-issued binding safety stop: code mapping proved the current Images `revision` and `hardExpiresAtMs` are caller supplied, per-descriptor digest and persisted `selectedAt` do not exist, provider/profile identities have no revisioned contract registry, and the only credential scope is a legacy catalog HMAC that depends on a local secret excluded from epoch-2 preservation. A provisional resolver that accepted parallel fact objects was deleted rather than branding self-reported provenance. Required order is now canonical descriptor digest/set revision -> epoch-2 credential scope and provider contract registry -> schema/repository facts -> private resolved binding.
- Canonical descriptor step in progress: the provider-owned V2 descriptor codec accepts only the official Images top-level `{id,endpoints}` envelope, projects every currently defined capability-bearing endpoint field into ordered arrays, and explicitly allows but excludes `pricing` as non-capability diagnostic data. Any other envelope/descriptor field blocks for contract review. It computes per-descriptor SHA-256 digests and a model-bound endpoint-set content revision, rejects duplicate tags/rules/allowlist entries and never accepts caller-supplied revision/expiry. It stores no raw diagnostic object and does not yet claim fetch, credential-scope, freshness or repository authority.
- Canonical descriptor verification: the versioned 2026-07-14 redacted exact descriptor audit decodes successfully; order invariance, capability-change invalidation, duplicate/malformed/accessor/unknown-field and envelope fallback rejection are covered. Final review reports P0=0/P1=0; canonical descriptor plus boundary tests pass 2 files / 10 tests.
- Credential-scope step in progress: a Node-only pure V2 primitive derives `credential-scope-v2:<HMAC-SHA256>` from a branded provider identity, the normalized current credential and an epoch-owned 32-byte scope key. Credential/provider/key rotation isolates scopes; Buffer-backed input is force-copied, SharedArrayBuffer is rejected, and a golden vector locks domain/order/4-byte-BE framing. Zeroization is best-effort for owned byte copies only: the original JavaScript credential string and Node/OpenSSL internal HMAC copies cannot be guaranteed erased. The output contains no API key or credential revision. Key generation, restricted epoch-2 persistence and repository wiring remain pending and no legacy catalog scope is accepted.
- Credential-scope verification: Buffer aliasing and missing cross-version vector P1 findings were fixed. Final review reports P0=0/P1=0; credential and boundary tests pass 2 files / 8 tests. The package remains Node-only and has no renderer, legacy or production caller.
- Epoch-key store attempt withdrawn before commit: review found that a fixed `.pending` file permits concurrent creators to delete each other's work, a caller-supplied cipher callback cannot prove Electron safe-storage encryption, and path-based `lstat` checks cannot close the junction/reparse TOCTOU window. No key-store production code is retained. Reintroduction requires a coordinator-held cross-process owner lease, a private verified Electron safe-storage adapter, per-attempt owned pending files, no-overwrite publication, and platform-native handle-relative/no-follow file operations with deterministic concurrency and path-swap tests.

## Gate 0 Owner decision packet

| Decision | Frozen value or plan-recommended value awaiting explicit approval |
|---|---|
| Canonical packaged identity | **Resolved:** production `io.github.guxinghai.starverse` / `Starverse` / `starverse-client`; only dev/E2E runtime AppUserModelID `.dev`/`.e2e` variants; ordinary launches in every channel explicitly share `%APPDATA%\Starverse`; no `com.starverse.desktop` recognition or migration |
| OpenAI Responses continuation | Client-managed complete native response/reasoning/encrypted/tool/image items; no `previous_response_id` or conversation fallback |
| Ollama/local protocol binding | Every endpoint profile requires one explicit protocol choice; no probing/fallback to another codec |
| OpenRouter beta server web tool | Disabled by default; explicit experimental opt-in and capability/evidence gate |
| Automatic transport retry | Disabled for every provider; attempt remains 1 until a separately approved provider/error idempotency matrix exists |
| Image continuation first release | Enable only official native edit/continuation contracts with complete artifacts; otherwise visibly unavailable; sibling regenerate remains separate |
| Anthropic `thinking.display` | **Resolved:** user-facing `provider_default|summarized|omitted`, default `summarized`; native continuation blocks/signatures/order are preserved for every display selection |
| OpenRouter first-party endpoint profile | Owner must freeze whether one immutable versioned profile covers Chat and Images or Images owns a separate profile, and provide the canonical profile ID; no legacy/test ID is inferred |

- 2026-07-17: Fresh-initializer mapping found two Owner-level boundaries that must be fixed before code. First, Electron `safeStorage.encryptString/decryptString` necessarily materializes the 32-byte scope key as an immutable JavaScript string; owned byte buffers can be zeroed immediately, but the transient string cannot be guaranteed erased before garbage collection. Avoiding that residual requires a different native byte-encryption contract rather than the frozen real-`safeStorage` path. Second, the existing schema composer owns and commits its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; requiring schema DDL/manifest plus `app_meta_v2` and `epoch_scope_key_envelope_v2` in one transaction requires a deliberate composer refactor, while requiring only the two identity rows to be atomic permits an explicit schema-only crash-recovery state. No production code for this unit has been written pending those decisions.
- 2026-07-17: Official evidence was refreshed without production changes. Electron's current [`safeStorage`](https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/safe-storage) contract still accepts plaintext only as `string`, returns ciphertext as `Buffer`, and decrypts to `string`; Windows availability begins after app ready and uses DPAPI. SQLite's [transaction contract](https://www.sqlite.org/lang_transaction.html) documents `CREATE` and `INSERT` as write statements inside explicit transactions and rejects nested `BEGIN`, so one complete schema/manifest/identity transaction is supported but requires the composer to stop owning a nested transaction. Recommended decision remains: accept the unavoidable short-lived string while minimizing its scope, and refactor the composer for one complete `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` rather than introducing a schema-only recovery state.

- 2026-07-17: Owner resumed Goal 2 and closed both fresh-initializer decisions. The implementation must use Electron main-process asynchronous `safeStorage`, accepting only its unavoidable short-lived plaintext JavaScript string while never logging, caching, IPC-exposing or renderer-exposing it and immediately zeroing every controllable Buffer. The logical recovered database state is binary: uninitialized or fully initialized; a nonempty main file, rollback journal or WAL may physically remain after a crash, but schema-only state is never accepted or repaired. One bootstrap-owned `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` atomically installs schema DDL, schema manifest, `app_meta_v2` and `epoch_scope_key_envelope_v2`; the schema composer must not own transaction control. Journal-mode and other transaction-prohibited PRAGMAs run and verify before `BEGIN`; post-commit reopen/readback must validate canonical appId, epoch, rootId, schema version/digest, manifest and decryptable canonical 32-byte envelope before any later activation. The prior blocker is closed.
- 2026-07-17: Re-verified the Owner's final wording against the implemented fresh initializer. Recovery acceptance is explicitly a logical invariant, never a zero-byte-file promise: SQLite may leave a nonempty main file, rollback journal or WAL, but reopen must classify the database only as empty/uninitialized or fully initialized and must reject rather than repair schema-only or identity-incomplete state. The async main-process `safeStorage` path remains fail-closed, does not log/cache/IPC-expose plaintext, validates canonical Base64 to exactly 32 bytes and zeroes every controllable Buffer. No production change was required by this clarification; the bootstrap remains zero-activation.
- 2026-07-17: Version verification found the repository and installed runtime are Electron 38.6.0, whose local `SafeStorage` type has only synchronous methods. Electron introduced asynchronous `safeStorage` in 42.0.0; the early 42 implementation had an availability/crash defect fixed in 42.4.1, and 42.7.0 is the current supported patch release. The Owner-required asynchronous boundary therefore cannot be truthfully implemented on Electron 38 or behind a sync fallback. Upgrade impact is being mapped before changing the package/runtime/native ABI baseline.
- 2026-07-17: Upgraded the local dependency and lockfile to Electron 42.7.0 and materialized its lazy-downloaded runtime with `npx electron --version`, which returned `v42.7.0`. The install removed Electron 38's obsolete downloader dependency subtree and did not change the already-resolved TypeScript 5.9.3 version. The first full TypeScript check exposed that the repository already uses ES2022 `Array.prototype.at()` broadly while the root compiler contract still declared ES2020; the compiler target/library are being aligned to ES2022 rather than rewriting valid runtime-supported calls. No initializer or startup activation has been added yet.
- 2026-07-17: Aligned both TypeScript compiler projects to ES2022, matching the already-used `Array.prototype.at()` API and the Node 22/Electron 42 runtime floor. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false` and `npx vue-tsc --noEmit` pass after the alignment.

The migrated schema/repository regression passes 11 files / 98 tests.
- 2026-07-17: Implemented the still-unactivated fresh epoch-2 database initializer. It binds Electron's real async `safeStorage` singleton directly in main-process code, requires async availability, generates a canonical Base64 encoding of 32 CSPRNG bytes, never returns or logs plaintext, and zeroes every controllable key/ciphertext buffer. It configures and verifies `journal_mode=DELETE`, foreign keys and `synchronous=FULL` before a single bootstrap-owned `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; schema DDL, manifest, `app_meta_v2` and `epoch_scope_key_envelope_v2` commit together. A post-commit readonly reopen verifies installed-object digest, schema digest/version, canonical appId, epoch/root identity, envelope shape and real decryption before returning a non-executable result. Existing schema with missing or mismatched identity is rejected without patch-up. Logical rollback recovery accepts an empty recovered schema regardless of whether SQLite left the physical main file, journal or WAL empty or nonempty.

Required direct consumers are the OpenAI action coordinators, snapshot commit, native request codec/compiler and exact-byte tests; legacy composer controls remain the source of the user interaction and are not to be simplified.
- 2026-07-18: Completed the epoch-2 managed attachment-byte authority prerequisite. The Win32 Node-API lease now stores only canonical digest refs under `epoch-2/assets/sha256/<prefix>/<sha256>` through handle-relative opens; it re-verifies the epoch manifest, rejects reparse points and non-single-link files, and validates byte count plus SHA-256 on every read. A V2 blob-store facade exposes no paths or generic file I/O, and issues the existing post-commit send-byte lease only after a snapshot revision resolves to those verified managed bytes. Native write is durable-before-metadata, so a database failure can leave only an unreferenced immutable blob; it cannot leave a database-referenced blob absent, and the destructive epoch reset remains the authoritative cleanup boundary. Focused Windows native/storage/repository tests pass 3/3, the Node native build and TypeScript pass. Existing composer, attachment UI and legacy paths were not changed. OpenAI Files upload, credential-scoped descriptor persistence and command/snapshot integration remain open.
- 2026-07-18: Advanced the dormant OpenAI Responses attachment vertical through all reviewed current-config actions and snapshot retry replay, without changing IPC or renderer surfaces. An exact `(credential scope, asset revision, asset SHA-256) -> user_data file_id` descriptor is immutable and separate from normal chat rows; native history resolves only that persisted descriptor into `input_file`, never a path, binary or data URL. Initial send, regenerate and edit-resend resolve/reuse descriptors before their graph commit, then persist descriptor id/revision/hash in the snapshot; retry copies that snapshot and recompiles the same `input_file` without rereading local attachment bytes or current attachment settings. Unsupported conversion/send shapes reject through the capability/compiler boundary rather than uploading or omitting them. File-upload response consumption is streaming and capped at 256 KiB, so an absent or dishonest content-length cannot force an unbounded `arrayBuffer()` allocation; non-2xx uploads reject before graph mutation. A command race can leave an unreferenced provider-file descriptor after a successful remote upload but before a later graph transaction is rejected; it cannot leave a committed snapshot without a descriptor, and the descriptor is safely reusable only for the same credential scope/revision/hash. This remains dormant: no production coordinator factory currently receives the epoch-2 blob store, and no IPC/UI route reaches V2. The next direct blocker is the parity-preserving runtime composition/IPC cutover that injects `Epoch2CommittedRuntime.attachmentBlobStore` and maps existing composer attachments into strict V2 intents; no existing attachment controls or layout may be reduced. Core acceptance passes 7 focused suites / 39 tests (`initial`, `regenerate`, `edit-resend`, `retry`, native byte authority, descriptor and request codec), TypeScript and diff checks pass. No user-reachable behavior is claimed.

- 2026-07-18: Added the first executable OpenRouter Images authority: `openRouterImageDescriptorAuthorityV2Service` is the sole owner of descriptor `GET /api/v1/images/models/{model}/endpoints` freshness. The generation runner will consume only its resolved record and cannot independently refresh/switch endpoints. TypeScript passes. Focused mocked authority tests and its command consumer remain the next work; no production IPC/UI route is claimed active.
- 2026-07-18: Extended the common runtime-capability codec with `dimensions_enum`: an exact, canonicalized set of `{width,height}` pairs. This is required for endpoint descriptors that publish discrete image sizes; converting those to min/max would falsely advertise unsupported Cartesian-product sizes, while hiding them would regress valid image settings. The existing range domain remains unchanged for providers that actually publish a continuous rectangle. Added a structural codec fixture for canonical ordering and exact round-trip. The local focused Vitest command again returned only the runner banner and is not counted as a pass; TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass. The Images capability authority will consume `dimensions_enum` only when the selected descriptor has a losslessly parseable `size` enum.
- 2026-07-18: Implemented the pure selected-descriptor capability composer at `providers/openrouter-images/imageRuntimeCapabilityV2.ts`. It accepts only a binding whose profile, contract, operation and selector exactly equal the selected descriptor, emits a full canonical `RuntimeCapabilitySnapshotV2`, and records separate contract/descriptor support/rejection evidence. It maps `n`, `seed` and supported image wire fields to their exact V2 domains, uses `dimensions_enum` only for parseable discrete `size` pairs, preserves the selected endpoint's streaming capability, and leaves image-reference attachment issuance unavailable for its separate authority. Descriptor enum values are intersected with the values expressible by the unified semantic intent; no unknown value is surfaced through a broadened common domain. Reasoning/web/tools/provider extensions remain disabled-only or unavailable as appropriate, so explicit unsupported settings reject before request bytes. TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass. Command transaction integration and focused composer tests remain next.
- 2026-07-18: Added the Images command inputs and binding/transaction prerequisites. `imageInitialSendCommandV2` accepts only graph identity, prompt, model, optional explicit provider tag and operation ID; all provider/profile/contract/capability facts stay main-process-owned. `imageProviderBindingV2` is the sole issuer of the pinned OpenRouter Images binding from the reviewed registry plus the permanent profile. `OpenRouterImageBindingRepo` now exposes an authority-context CAS entry; its implementation executes as a SQLite savepoint inside the outer V2 `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, preserving the outer all-or-nothing commit/rollback boundary. TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass. The coordinator must perform descriptor refresh before entering that synchronous transaction, then resolve current config/attachments and apply selection/CAS/snapshot/request in it; no transaction-scoped config authority may cross `await`.
- 2026-07-18: Corrected two pre-coordinator Images boundaries. Initial-send commands now carry the complete command-owned attachment intent through a provider-neutral attachment codec, so a selected `image_reference` cannot disappear between UI command and snapshot/preflight; the subsequent image-reference issuer remains an explicit prerequisite and must reject unavailable references rather than erase them. The binding repository’s authority-context CAS now executes directly in the caller-owned V2 transaction rather than creating a nested `better-sqlite3` savepoint, so the future command can atomically commit graph choice/head, snapshot, prepared request and binding under one transaction owner. The added command test and static/diff checks were invoked once for this slice; TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass, while the local Vitest process again produced no result summary and is not counted as a pass.

The independent Images compiler accepts only that `image_generate` snapshot plus its already-selected descriptor set, emits the exact `/api/v1/images` prepared body and `provider.only` pin with a single-attempt bearer plan, and has no branch to Chat. Mount-time UI preference reads were then moved off the retired `db:invoke` settings handlers onto a dedicated `generationV2UiPreferences` namespace in the retained, renderer-safe Electron config store. These values are presentation/default preferences only; they do not contain credentials, messages, snapshots or request semantics. The old chat database is not reintroduced to satisfy the UI. Remaining activation work includes replacing the legacy model-catalog controls and removing any other secondary UI invocation of unregistered legacy IPC.
- The V2 preload model bridge now exposes the five already-registered provider-owned availability contracts (OpenRouter, OpenAI Responses, Anthropic, Google AI Studio and DeepSeek). This is not a generic transport or catalog wire adapter: every method maps to one fixed provider operation IPC. The existing picker still uses its retired scoped-catalog query client, so the next working slice must replace that client while retaining its UI filters/sorting/detail layout; no false activation claim is made yet.
- Replaced the picker query and refresh authority with the five fixed V2 availability bridges. The renderer now projects only provider-returned model facts into the retained filtering, sorting and paging UI; it does not reopen the old catalog database or infer an operation protocol. Filters without a V2 evidence field remain visibly unavailable instead of being silently approximated. The existing endpoint-detail and catalog-cache maintenance actions still require their own V2 projection/removal and are not claimed as cut over. `npx vite build --mode development --config vite.config.ts` passed after this change (only baseline-browser-mapping, Browserslist-age and chunk-size warnings).
- The model-detail and endpoint-detail projections now reuse that same V2 availability query rather than invoking the retired scoped-catalog bridge. The obsolete `db:modelCatalogSynced` renderer listener was removed because the V2 model source is an explicit refresh/query, not an old database event. Legacy cache-maintenance controls remain explicitly outstanding; V2 must not recreate `chat.db` catalog caches merely to keep their old IPC callable.
- Settings-level OpenRouter validation now invokes the same V2 provider-owned model availability contract after credential update. The retained catalog-cache controls no longer invoke unregistered legacy cache IPC or mutate an old workspace; they explain that epoch-2 has no model-catalog cache to clear. `npx vite build --mode development --config vite.config.ts` passed after this cutover, with only the pre-existing browser-data/chunk-size warnings.
- Removed the remaining model-picker subscription to the retired `db:modelCatalogSynced` event. V2 list freshness is now entirely produced by the explicit provider-owned availability query at picker open or user refresh; no legacy database event is required or listened for.
- Corrected the V2 model-catalog cursor boundary: the next-page cursor now represents the final item returned by the current page, preventing the first model of a following page from being skipped. This is renderer-side pagination only; it does not alter provider model facts or introduce a cache.
- Removed an unreachable legacy draft-attachment send-plan fallback left after the V2 composer early return. More importantly, the currently reachable image “retry preview” control now reads the exact epoch-2 managed revision through `generation-v2:composer:read-preview`; it no longer invokes legacy `preview.getLatestReady` or `preview.ensure`. The V2 preview is a direct managed-blob projection, so it does not create derivative-job fallback state. `npx vite build --mode development --config vite.config.ts` passed after the removal and bridge change, with only existing browser-data/chunk-size warnings.
- Completed the V2 history-attachment display projection. Branch reads now join managed attachment revisions to immutable filename, asset kind, MIME and source-kind facts; no managed URL provenance or URL-reference value is exposed. The renderer renders a chosen answer snapshot’s attachments under its user question directly from that V2 projection, reading image previews by exact managed revision. A URL reference is explicitly rendered as a localized “URL reference” label and never reveals its full URL in historical UI. The legacy attachment tables are not queried on this V2 branch path. `npx vite build --mode development --config vite.config.ts` passed, with only existing browser-data/chunk-size warnings.
- Began concentrated OpenRouter Images core acceptance after runtime activation. `npm run rebuild:node` completed, then the nine focused V2 descriptor/selection/request/stream/capability/command tests exposed three strict-boundary regressions: a missing `endpoint_profile_digest` identity registration, invalid `official_documentation` evidence source (protocol ID rather than verified HTTPS provenance URL), and fixtures stale after `AttachmentIntentV2.kind` became mandatory. Corrected all without widening any decoder; the image capability fixture now uses the actual reviewed registry contract rather than a fabricated digest. The full focused set passed 41/42 before that fixture correction, and the corrected capability test passed 2/2. The final all-nine rerun remains pending for the next validation batch.
- The final OpenRouter Images core rerun passed: 9 files / 42 tests (descriptor canonicalization/freshness, user endpoint binding, capability, intent projection, request shape, stream decoding and initial command). Initial 13-file run found two tests that had not migrated with current V2 contracts: a managed attachment fixture lacked `kind`, and the native-boundary assertion still assumed bootstrap had no legal consumers despite the intentional application-runtime composition and typed V2 IPC injection. This preserves the Owner-approved `link_only -> url_reference` no-download/no-fallback boundary.

- Reconciled the direct attachment repository suite with the now-explicit attachment discriminator and current immutable conversion schema. Managed-file fixtures now declare `kind: "managed_file"`; the derived-revision assertion recognizes the schema’s valid immutable `(parent revision, converter contract, converter revision)` provenance while retaining the stronger request-authority check that an unissued conversion cannot be sent. `infra/db/repo/attachmentAssetV2Repo.test.ts` passes 11/11. The focused epoch attachment-blob test passed 3/3 and the OpenAI Responses generation suite passed 20/20 in the preceding combined run.
- Corrected a native-history terminal-authority defect found by static integration: generation request repository facts deliberately omit mutable request state, so Generic/local OpenAI Chat, LM Studio OpenResponses and Ollama history repos now read the authoritative request row inside the existing generation authority transaction before persisting an operation-terminal native artifact. They require both operation and request state to be `completed`; no mutable state was added to the immutable fact or inferred from UI/stream state.
- Continued the post-cutover static-integration cleanup without treating TypeScript diagnostics as cosmetic. Anthropic continuation idempotency no longer compares the removed legacy `userConfirmedExternalSideEffect` pseudo-column: it now verifies the persisted V2 side-effect policy, confirmation state and confirmation timestamp against the submitted confirmation decision. Anthropic compiler, authority, snapshot and stream terminal paths now also explicitly normalize an absent optional tools intent to the only safe semantic default (`disabled`) before inspecting it. This avoids both an unchecked runtime dereference and a false tool-use continuation acceptance. Focused validation is deliberately batched with the remaining provider type fixes; the outstanding full `vue-tsc` blocker remains open.
- Focused validation for that Anthropic repair passed: `npx vitest --run electron/services/anthropicPlainTextGenerationActionsV2.test.ts electron/services/anthropicPlainTextSnapshotCommitV2.test.ts infra/db/repo/anthropicNativeHistoryV2Repo.test.ts` completed 3 files / 22 tests. Node ABI was already current; no rebuild, broad static gate or Electron smoke was run.
- The deliberately deferred renderer static gate is now green: `npx vue-tsc --noEmit --pretty false` completed successfully with no diagnostic output after the attachment-discriminator, native-history terminal-authority, OpenRouter identity and Anthropic continuation integrations. - Replaced the user-reachable SearchModal's retired `dbBridge` search calls with a fixed epoch-2 `generation-v2:search:*` IPC path. The preserved UI still supports project/conversation/message scope, exact/fuzzy query, filters, snippets, relevance ordering and explicit index rebuild; its repository reads only `project_v2`, `conversation_v2`, `message_v2` and `message_body_v2`. The new FTS5 schema keeps those documents current through exact project/conversation/message/body terminal triggers, and the composer now treats the five fixed FTS5 shadow tables as a verified internal set rather than weakening its closed-schema check. This is a real parity gap, not an acceptable disabled/fallback state. - Repaired the active DeepSeek stable capability-policy import blocker exposed by the focused command-chain test. `AttachmentIntentV2` had added managed/URL-reference semantic paths and the shared capability universe had added new sampling, provider-extension and web paths, but DeepSeek's reviewed stable contract omitted explicit rules for them. The policy now records every new path as either unsupported or unavailable pending provider authority; it does not authorize a URL, file, sampling, extension or web field by omission. The DeepSeek stable policy, model authority, initial send, snapshot commit, retry/replace/as-new/regenerate/edit-resend, IPC and native-history focused suite passes 6 files / 52 tests after the current schema-aware authority assertion was updated. No generic fallback or transport behavior changed.
- Migrated the user-reachable plugin-management lifecycle off `dbBridge` and the legacy DB worker. Epoch-2 now owns a closed registry schema fragment and `mainV2` constructs one lifecycle-service singleton over the committed epoch database, verified epoch plugin/runtime roots and the Electron-aware official package transport. The retained panel/client decoders now call fixed `generation-v2:plugins:*` channels; install status, cancellation, enable/disable, uninstall, health, official/local registration and diagnostics preserve their existing service semantics. LibreOffice package import remains deliberately dialog-only: renderer never receives a raw package-path endpoint, while dialog IPC invokes the epoch singleton callback. V2 plugin IPC rejects arrays, non-plain objects and unknown command fields before service execution. Focused schema/client/IPC/network-transport validation passes 4 files / 40 tests; the unchanged panel suite passes 31 tests; the development Vite renderer/main/preload build passes. Existing browser-data age, Node sqlite externalization and chunk-size notices are non-failing environment/bundle warnings. Legacy worker handler/registry migration files are no longer used by the V2 runtime, but repository deletion remains part of final legacy-removal acceptance.
- Proxy-setting follow-up is explicitly blocked on a product semantic decision, not implementation uncertainty. The retained settings UI exposes legacy `environment/manual/direct/system` values, while the epoch-2 Electron session policy has `system/direct/fixed_servers/pac_script/auto_detect`. In particular, mapping `environment` to Electron `system` changes its source from process environment variables to OS/Electron system resolution. No automatic mapping, fallback transport or silent mode substitution will be added. Until the owner chooses a new policy UI or an exact environment-mode contract, plugin downloads use the Electron-owned transport but the old proxy-settings UI is not claimed as V2-cut-over.
- 2026-07-19 (blocked design audit): Audited the remaining user-reachable legacy branch context/filter surface. The active V2 send command no longer consumes `context.buildForBranch`, but the answer UI still reads/writes legacy `branchFilter` state. A proposed branch-local epoch-2 filter relation was deliberately not retained: V2 provider-native continuation strictly replays the immediate native artifact; excluding an ancestor answer would require either forbidden reconstruction from visible text or a branch/history rewrite. A UI-only filter would falsely claim request behavior. The schema composer core suite (17/17) was run during this investigation; no V2 filter schema, IPC or UI change remains. Required product/architecture decision: whether exclusion is forbidden when it intersects required native lineage, or exclusion explicitly creates a new branch/history boundary. No silent omission or provider-specific fallback will be used.

Found and corrected the OpenRouter Images regenerate attachment-source defect: its command is now deliberately attachment-free, so renderer-supplied current-draft attachments cannot be silently ignored; within the authority transaction, regenerate loads exact references from the committed chosen-answer snapshot. Current provider/config resolution remains current-config; attachment reference strings remain historical snapshot facts. A focused command test was added; command-to-wire and retry/regenerate execution tests remain required before this is marked complete.
- 2026-07-19: The bounded OpenRouter Images command/codec group passes 3 files / 8 tests: initial command keeps explicit command attachments, regenerate rejects any renderer-supplied attachment field and has no attachment field in its fingerprint, and the exact `input_references` codec remains pinned. No Node rebuild, broad static gate or Electron smoke was run.
- 2026-07-19: `npx vite build --mode development --config vite.config.ts` passes after the regenerate command-boundary correction. It built renderer, `epoch2MainEntry`, `mainV2` and preload. Baseline-browser-mapping/Browserslist age, Electron externalized `node:sqlite` in Undici and existing large-chunk notices are non-failing bundle warnings; no ABI rebuild or Electron smoke was run.
- 2026-07-19 (open vertical): The retained New Chat template remains reachable in the UI but still depends on the legacy system-template worker transaction for template draft/config/reset, idempotent materialization, first-turn creation and lifecycle settings. Epoch-2 currently has no hidden/system conversation discriminator, so aliasing its ordinary default conversation would leak the template into project lists and break the old materialization invariant. This must be migrated as one dedicated V2 template persistence + command + UI vertical, not hidden behind a legacy bridge or a partial renderer alias.
- 2026-07-19 (Owner decision, supersedes prior context-filter blocker): Withdraw the proposed provider-continuation-segment and branch-required designs. The retained branch-local context filter is a request-time, complete-turn projection: it does not alter the message tree, create a branch, fork, create hidden continuation state or rebuild visible text into native history. Question or chosen-answer exclusion excludes the whole completed turn, including its provider-native bundle. For every send, the V2 authority must resolve the branch path and chosen answers, apply the branch filter, validate every included native turn bundle, record the resulting included/excluded turn projection and digest in the command snapshot, then compile the complete client-managed history through the current provider's typed codec. When a filter is active, all server-managed continuation handles (including `previous_response_id` and `previous_interaction_id`) are forbidden. Only an incomplete included native bundle, an active/incomplete tool loop, unsupported native-history expression, or a provider codec incapability may reject the command; no automatic branch or fallback is permitted. This restores the Owner-approved model-visible-history semantics while preserving the existing UI controls and layout.
- 2026-07-19 (in progress): Began the V2 context-filter vertical from the retained UI rather than restoring `dbBridge`. `branch_context_filter_v2` is graph-independent and stores only branch-local question/chosen-answer visibility rules; its trigger validates completed path questions and terminal chosen answers. `ConversationReadV2Repo` now returns each turn's effective visibility, and the existing retained UI projection consumes it. Added fixed epoch-2 workspace IPC/preload/client commands for set/clear; the existing question/answer controls now call those commands, so neither action writes legacy `branch_filter`. Operation creation now atomically stores an immutable `generation_context_projection_v2` record (turn modes, selected answers, included message IDs and digest) with the assistant snapshot. Unsupported contracts are rejected as `CONTEXT_PROJECTION_UNSUPPORTED_BY_PROVIDER`; OpenAI Responses is the first contract being moved to a complete selected-native-bundle compiler, so it is the only provisional exception to that rejection. Focused repository/schema validation passes 2 files / 20 tests.
- 2026-07-19 (in progress): OpenAI Responses context projection now has a dedicated exact replay-input codec path, mutually exclusive with adjacent-artifact continuation. Filter-active history resolves the immutable operation projection and derives each included completed turn from operation-terminal native artifacts, retaining user input, encrypted reasoning, tool calls/results and hosted-tool items as one bundle. The request compiler submits those selected replay items directly with `store:false`, never `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, or a synthetic continuation artifact. The first projected request terminal persists a parent-less cumulative artifact; subsequent tool-continuation requests remain native request chains within that same answer. Later bundle reconstruction uses the immutable operation projection rather than hidden segment state. The native history loader still needs focused integration tests for selected middle-turn exclusion, tool-loop replay and malformed projection rejection before OpenAI can be claimed as a completed provider vertical.
- 2026-07-19: Focused persistence and exact-body checks after the projection change pass: `generationExecutionV2Repo` 9/9 verifies operation/snapshot/projection atomic persistence and idempotent replay, while the OpenAI Responses request codec 7/7 verifies the mutually exclusive explicit replay-input form and absence of server history handles. The larger OpenAI stream/history integration run was started but its terminal summary was not returned by the local runner, so it is intentionally not counted as evidence.
- 2026-07-19 (test added, pending runner evidence): Added the OpenAI runtime integration case that creates Q1/A1 and Q2/A2, excludes A2 through the V2 filter repository, then asserts Q3's exact prepared `input` contains only Q1/A1/Q3 and the new immutable projection records the excluded mode. Local Vitest did not return a terminal summary for this service suite, so this case is not yet counted as passing evidence and needs a bounded rerun/diagnosis in the next validation batch.
- 2026-07-19 (in progress): Replaced the temporary contract-ID exception for request-time context filtering with an explicit reviewed-contract policy. `contextProjectionPolicy` is now a provider-contract fact: OpenAI Responses alone currently declares `complete_turn_client_managed_replay`; every other reviewed contract declares `unsupported` until its own typed codec can losslessly project complete native bundles. The generation transaction consults the immutable snapshot contract revision rather than a hard-coded provider branch. OpenAI selected-history loading now reports `CONTEXT_TURN_NATIVE_BUNDLE_INCOMPLETE` for a missing, malformed, cyclic, prefix-inconsistent, active, or incomplete included turn bundle. The projected terminal artifact is intentionally parent-less and preserves only the selected full bundle prefix plus the current turn; it is not a hidden continuation segment. Added focused unit and integration cases, but the local Vitest runner again produced no terminal summary for this slice, so no new passing-test claim is made here.
- 2026-07-19 (in progress): Extended the same capability-gated complete-turn projection to DeepSeek Stable Chat. Its exact codec now has a mutually exclusive `replayEntries` form, so a filtered request sends selected provider-native messages directly rather than a cumulative parent artifact. DeepSeek repository projection retains the root system message independently of turn filtering, extracts each selected turn from its verified terminal native artifact, keeps `reasoning_content` and complete tool-call/tool-result cycles intact, and rejects an invalid selected bundle as `CONTEXT_TURN_NATIVE_BUNDLE_INCOMPLETE`. A filtered terminal artifact is parent-less by design; intra-answer tool continuations retain their normal native request sequence. DeepSeek is therefore declared `complete_turn_client_managed_replay` by the reviewed contract; the remaining contracts stay explicitly unsupported. Added codec regression coverage; runtime/repository integration validation is intentionally deferred to the consolidated focused validation batch.

- 2026-07-19 (in progress): OpenRouter Chat now has the same request-time projection seam. The native-history repository derives each eligible selected question/answer turn as a verified cumulative-artifact delta, keeps the system root outside the turn filter, validates tool-call/tool-result closure, and builds an explicit selected-message prefix. The prepared request compiler consumes that prefix directly; the terminal runner derives the current-turn delta from the exact persisted body and persists a parent-less selected-history artifact. This is an explicit `complete_turn_client_managed_replay` contract capability, not generic Chat Completions message slicing. Focused exact-body/repository tests remain to be run in the consolidated context-projection validation batch.

- 2026-07-19 (in progress): Anthropic Messages context projection is now reconstructed from provider-native persistence rather than the prior visible-parent walk. For every included completed turn, the repository reads the final request sequence, replays each request-terminal assistant block in order, and inserts the exact persisted tool-result user block after each `tool_use`; final `thinking`, `redacted_thinking`, signatures, block order and `is_error` values are retained through the existing native codecs. The context projection contributes only selected complete turns plus the current user message; the system root remains outside the filter. This corrects the former loss of earlier completed tool loops and authorizes Anthropic's reviewed `complete_turn_client_managed_replay` capability. Focused repository/tool-loop regression coverage remains required before acceptance.
- 2026-07-19 (in progress): Gemini GenerateContent now consumes the persisted operation projection as explicit `replayContents`, never an adjacent cumulative artifact. Every included completed answer is checked against its own immutable projection and yields its terminal native `user`/`model` turn; excluded turns are absent, the current question is appended as the final `user` content, and the selected terminal artifact is an explicit parent-less root. This is safe for the currently implemented single-request/no-tool GenerateContent surface; future Gemini tool continuation must extend the extractor to a complete multi-request native bundle rather than reuse this two-content rule. The reviewed GenerateContent contract now explicitly declares `complete_turn_client_managed_replay`; Interactions remains unsupported pending its independent typed implementation.
- 2026-07-19 (critical correctness repair): Gemini terminal persistence had been structurally unable to commit: `generation_native_artifact_v2` requires the JSON envelope's `artifactCodecVersion` and `artifactHash` to equal its columns, while GenerateContent history artifacts omitted the former and response diagnostics omitted both. The native history codec now persists `artifactCodecVersion: 1`; the response artifact computes its digest over the closed projection and persists the resulting envelope hash. Both writes remain inside the existing terminal transaction, so no completed answer can appear without its required native artifacts. Focused Gemini codec/repository/runner tests remain to be added and run in the consolidated validation batch.
- 2026-07-19 (static checkpoint): `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false` completed with no diagnostic output after the Gemini explicit-replay and artifact-envelope changes. - 2026-07-19 (in progress): Generic/local OpenAI Chat is now a centrally reviewed fixed protocol rather than manufacturing a parallel one-entry registry revision. Its local profile binding derives revision/digest from the central registry, which unblocks command projection validation. The history repository now resolves the immutable operation projection, independently retains a system root, and replay-compiles only selected verified terminal `user`/`assistant` bundles plus the current user message. It also corrects two dormant-path defects: provider/contract identity is read from request sequence 1 rather than nonexistent snapshot columns, and artifact persistence uses the canonical serializer. Ollama remains explicitly unsupported for filtering: its current stream can terminate with a dangling native tool call and has no full tool-result continuation, so accepting turn omission there would violate the native-bundle invariant.

- 2026-07-19 (LM Studio selected-turn projection, in progress): Replaced that temporary rejection with a typed complete-turn projection for the fixed `lmstudio-openresponses` contract. A filter-active request now replays only included terminal `[user, assistant-native-items]` turn bundles plus the current user item; it cannot use `previous_response_id` or any hidden segment. The terminal native artifact is parent-less and contains exactly the submitted selected replay plus the returned items. The extractor rejects missing/nonterminal artifacts, a current-question mismatch, malformed replay items, or a bundle that does not end in the expected input/output pair. - 2026-07-19 (Gemini Interactions SSE schema extraction): Directly parsed the pinned official OpenAPI rather than inferring from examples. The closed stream union requires `step.start(index,step)`, `step.delta(index,delta)`, `step.stop(index)` plus created/completed/status/error lifecycle events and `[DONE]`. `StepDeltaData` is a broad discriminated union; image deltas may carry `data` or `uri` and multiple media MIME values even though requested `ImageResponseFormat.mime_type` is JPEG-only. This proves the current native-step model (text/user/model/thought/function only) cannot losslessly persist the response, and that any eventual runner must atomically store ordered raw typed step/event output before projecting visible text/image. No partial decoder/runner was added because it would lose output association or silently drop schema variants.

- 2026-07-22 (Authorized normal-profile rebootstrap preflight — stopped before mutation): Owner explicitly authorized the destructive managed-root reinitialization described above. Preflight found that the implemented journal authority can resume only an unfinished operation: `writeEpoch2ResetJournalAtomic` requires the same `operationId` and permits only same/next-phase writes, while `runEpoch2ResetThroughConfigReplacement` accepts only `prepared|legacy_files_deleted|config_replaced`. A previously `committed` journal therefore has no legal transition to a new operation's `prepared` state. No production API currently retires a completed journal and atomically begins a later reset cycle. Manually deleting the journal, editing its JSON or deleting `workspace` would bypass the approved lease, crash-recovery and ownership boundary, so no database, journal, credential, config or workspace mutation was performed. Correct implementation requires an explicit main-owned reset-cycle rollover authority that accepts only `committed -> new prepared`, under the same lease and closed inventory, with crash-safe retirement/replacement and focused recovery tests; this newly discovered production gap is a blocker before the authorized rebootstrap can execute.

- 2026-07-22 (Model catalog sync failure investigation): Read-only inspection of the fresh epoch-2 database shows all five first-party catalog scopes have configured credential-scope IDs but `sync_state=error`, `last_error_code=network_error`, and no snapshots; this rules out missing credentials, parser failure, or a provider-specific model-list contract as the common cause. The retained `config.json` contains all five first-party credential records, while `networkProxySettingsV2` is absent because the destructive epoch projection preserves only the approved shell preferences and provider credentials. The product proxy therefore starts from its default `environment` mode; the current process has none of `http_proxy/HTTP_PROXY/https_proxy/HTTPS_PROXY`, so `compileProductNetworkProxyV2` returns `proxy_environment_unavailable`, startup marks governed cloud networking blocked, and `cloudFetch` rejects before any provider GET. The model-source adapters collapse that transport failure to `network_error`, the catalog IPC persists the generic code, and the picker falls back to its generic sync-failed label. No production code or provider request was made in this investigation; next action is an explicit proxy-mode/configuration choice or a separately approved error-propagation repair.
